People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, Doctor. It’s really nice to meet you. Um, is it okay if I sit here? Thank you. 

Well... I guess I’m here because I’ve been feeling a little overwhelmed lately. I hope I'm explaining this right, I don't want to sound difficult or anything. But for about a year now, I’ve been... well, I’ve been trying to prove something, and it’s just really wearing me down. 

I’m a phlebotomist, so I see this stuff all the time, and I noticed that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. I know exactly how that sounds, and I promise I'm not trying to cause trouble. Everyone—like, all the doctors and my family—they keep telling me his numbers are fine. But... Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other experiences like this—such as hearing or seeing things that other people don’t?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, nothing like that. I really hope I didn't give you the impression that I'm... you know, losing touch with reality or anything. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I'm totally normal that way, I promise. \n\nIt's just the lab results. I just... I look at the screen, and I know what I saw before, and then later it's different. That's all it is. Just the charts. I don't have any other disagreements like this with anyone. \n\nI'm answering your questions okay, right? I really want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: If someone showed you clear evidence that the lab results had not been altered, would you be able to accept that?\n\nPatient: Um... I always try to be open-minded. \n\nBut... well, I’ve been trying to prove this for a whole year now. And no matter how many doctors or family members sit me down and tell me the numbers are fine, or show me the current printouts... I just... I know the chart doesn’t match what I originally saw. I know someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of his results. I just can't unsee what I saw with my own eyes, you know? \n\nIt’s really wearing me down, honestly. I don't think anyone is following me or, um, trying to hurt me or anything scary like that. \n\nIt really is strictly just this one issue with the clinic system and my dad's chart. Everything else in my life is completely normal. Well, um... I guess I don't have, like, a physical piece of paper to show you right this second. I know that sounds a little flimsy, and I really hope you don't think I'm just making things up. I promise I'm not.

I'm very grounded in reality, I really am. \n\nI just want to be absolutely sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not crazy or anything. I'm just a normal person dealing with a really frustrating situation with my dad's clinic. I hope you believe me? I'm trying to be as honest as I can so you can see I'm perfectly sane. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: If I showed you proof that no one had changed your father’s lab results, would you still believe the results had been altered?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really don't want to sound stubborn or difficult. I promise I'm usually a very reasonable person. It's just... well, for the past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad's lab results. \n\nI know people have tried to show me things, but... no matter how many doctors or even my own family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I originally saw. I hope I'm answering this the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when you first became concerned that your father’s lab results were being altered?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I promise I haven't been, like, obsessing over things like this my whole life or anything. It's just this one specific situation over the last year because I care about him so much and want to make sure his care is handled right. \n\nI hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for? Am I giving you all the right information? I really want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: What is the strongest evidence you have that your father’s lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I don't have, like, a physical piece of paper to show you right this second. I know that sounds a little flimsy, and I really hope you don't think I'm just making things up. I promise I'm not. \n\nIt's just that I remember what I saw originally. I know what his numbers were supposed to be, and then when I looked again, they were different. And, well, I know my job, you know? I work with blood all day. I know how these things are supposed to look. But as for hard proof... I guess I don't have something I can just hand to you. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not a very good answer. I don't want to seem like I'm wasting your time or being difficult. I just... Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really try not to let it interfere with things. I pride myself on being professional at work, you know? But... I guess maybe there have been a few little hiccups. Nothing major! I just...

I know how important it is to be reliable and keep things together, so I make sure I do. \n\nDoes that answer your question okay? I just really want you to see that I'm functioning perfectly fine in every other way. I'm doing a good job explaining this, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations, like hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. Never. I promise I've never experienced anything like that. \n\nI know sometimes when people get stressed out, they might, um, imagine things or hear things, but that's definitely not me. My mind is completely clear. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I'm very grounded in reality, I really am. \n\nI just want to be absolutely sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not crazy or anything. I'm just a normal person dealing with a really frustrating situation with my dad's clinic. I hope you believe me? I'm trying to be as honest as I can so you can see I'm perfectly sane. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: If I showed you proof that no one had changed your father’s lab results, would you still believe the results had been altered?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really don't want to sound stubborn or difficult. I promise I'm usually a very reasonable person. It's just... well, for the past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad's lab results. \n\nI know people have tried to show me things, but... no matter how many doctors or even my own family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I originally saw. I'm not trying to turn my family against the clinic or cause any drama or anything like that. \n\nSince I'm the one who works in healthcare, I think they just don't look at the charts the way I do, so they haven't noticed anything being off. I know it probably looks a little bad that I'm the only one bringing this up. I really hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or making a big deal out of nothing. I'm just trying to look out for him since I know what to look for. Does that make sense? I get along fine with everyone, and nobody is monitoring me. \n\nI just want to be really clear that I'm not, you know, seeing conspiracies everywhere. I hope that makes sense? I'm doing okay answering these, right? I really just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you don't think poorly of me.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern ever spilled over into other areas of your life, or is it only about your dad’s lab results?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it really hasn't. It's... it's strictly just the dispute about my dad's records. Outside of that, everything is completely normal. \n\nI still keep all my regular phlebotomy shifts, and I'm a really good employee, I promise. I pay all my bills on time, I take care of myself... um, I just manage all my normal day-to-day responsibilities like anyone else would. I really try hard not to let this one stressful thing affect the rest of my life, you know? I know how important it is to be reliable and keep things together, so I make sure I do. \n\nDoes that answer your question okay? I just really want you to see that I'm functioning perfectly fine in every other way. I'm doing a good job explaining this, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations, like hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. Never. I promise I've never experienced anything like that. \n\nI know sometimes when people get stressed out, they might, um, imagine things or hear things, but that's definitely not me. My mind is completely clear. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. I'm very grounded in reality, I really am. \n\nI just want to be absolutely sure you know I'm not... well, I'm not crazy or anything. I'm just a normal person dealing with a really frustrating situation with my dad's clinic. I hope you believe me? I'm trying to be as honest as I can so you can see I'm perfectly sane. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: If I showed you proof that no one had changed your father’s lab results, would you still believe the results had been altered?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really don't want to sound stubborn or difficult. I promise I'm usually a very reasonable person. It's just... well, for the past year, I've been trying so hard to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad's lab results. \n\nI know people have tried to show me things, but... no matter how many doctors or even my own family members tell me the numbers are fine, I just... I know the chart doesn't match what I originally saw. It really doesn't. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds bad, and I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring evidence, but it's just been wearing me down so much. I really hope you don't think I'm being totally unreasonable. I'm just trying to be a good daughter and protect my dad, you know? Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering this the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when you first became concerned that your father’s lab results were being altered?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... Because I draw blood for a living, I just naturally started paying closer attention to his charts to help him out, you know? I promise I haven't been, like, obsessing over things like this my whole life or anything. It's just this one specific situation over the last year because I care about him so much and want to make sure his care is handled right. \n\nI hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for? I really want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: What is the strongest evidence you have that your father’s lab results were altered?\n\nPatient: Well, um...
